Also open from the plan: 5 of 47 linked torus rings arrive with ≤4 raw points (one 3-point ring) and `refineSliceRing` runs all 8 rounds on them, and the plan's claim that WIP 2dc7b3aa "touches mappers.js" is wrong (`git show --stat` = `scene3d.js` + `scene-xray-needs-fill.test.js`).
- W-27c **REJECTED** by adversarial review (verdict on the production diff `2dc7b3aa`, not on the test-only commit `29203162` — the 5 new tests are honest and are to be KEPT); implementer resumed for iteration 2. What the review settled: items (a) and (c)'s numbers are independently re-derived and not inflated (item a old 0.9641 mm → new 9.83e-10 mm over 1872 samples; item c cone 0.2762→3.84e-11, cylinder 0.0940→9.22e-10, torus 0.1756→8.76e-12), the bars (0.15 mm / <0.1 mm) match accepted precedent rather than being widened, the RED is faithful (the test's inline `oldEllipsoidProjectLocal` baseline is **byte-identical** to 55ddb720's real sphere-branch body), the plane-count purity tests are untouched byte-for-byte, and 59/59 guard tests pass. **Blocking defect (new, in neither prior report): `sliceAnalyticProjectLocal`'s Newton loop has no step clamp, no grew-worse rejection and no fallback when the in-plane-projected gradient is small but above the `denom < 1e-12` break** — i.e. wherever the cutting plane is near-tangent to the surface, which is exactly a ring's extremal points. Measured on the real wired API: eps 1e-3 → the point moves **106 mm** (F 0.10→39.3), 1e-4 → **1060 mm**, 1e-5 → **10600 mm**, and below ~3e-6 the point is returned silently unrefined. No NaN, no crash — a silently wrong point goes straight into the rendered ring.
- W-27c iteration-2 requirements (from the review): (1) **blocking** — add a bounds/sanity fallback to `sliceAnalyticProjectLocal` (reject a step whose resulting |F| grew, clamp the step, or fall back to the raw/previous point when `denom` is small-but-nonzero) and cover it with a near-tangent test; (2) **blocking to re-close item (c)** — add at least one cone/cylinder/torus case WITH object rotation and plane tilt, because mutation testing showed the current identity-only guards are materially weaker than advertised: deleting the in-loop gradient-onto-plane projection (the fix's own headline mechanism) leaves 24/24 green, and deleting all in-loop plane awareness still leaves all three item-(c) tests green (only the ellipsoid test catches it, at 0.298 mm); (3) non-blocking — the `maxTurnDeg` metric must be made open-polyline-aware before it is used to claim or deny item (b).
- W-27c item (b) contradiction RESOLVED by the review: the cone rings at the near-apex plane are **open arcs**, and both the implementer's and the reviewer's first `maxTurnDeg` applied modulo-n wraparound, manufacturing a phantom 112–140° "turn" across the two disconnected ends — an edge never drawn. The real internal maximum on that ring is **39.8°**, at a two-points-0.08 mm-apart near-duplicate cluster, not at the visually dominant tip. So the screenshots were right and the metric was wrong; item (b) nevertheless stays OPEN because 39.8° still exceeds the plan's ≤8°/≤12° bar. Any future cone/cylinder/torus angle assertion must be open-polyline-aware or it will keep reporting phantom defects — and note the separate W-27c-0/W-29 finding that the true analytic plane∩torus curve legitimately turns 95–108° per mm near the equator, so the angle bar itself needs restating before it is used as an oracle.
- A3 **PLAN-UNDER-JUDGEMENT** (`docs/3d-audit/lane-reports/A3-plan.md`, STATUS MEASURED; planner read-only, `handoff-c` worktree never edited, all instrumentation in a scratch export of 79c98ca8; an **Opus judge is running** → `A3-judge.md`). The standing A3 lead — PenFill/boolean-erosion **dropping** geometry — is refuted by measurement: across a whole torus render there are 0 boolean failures for four of the five self-crossing laws and exactly 1 for `trochoidLoop`, no erosion call anywhere returned empty because of a failure, and **0.000 mm² of the 0.89–2.06 mm² `ringNotInkMm2` is dropped geometry**. The plan's mechanism instead is **pen-unreachability**: 89–98% of each law's number sits in cells no 0.3 mm pen can ink while staying inside the *clipped* ribbon (convex corners and sub-2-pen necks), and a non-self-crossing control is 85% unreachable too. Excluding that set, all five laws already sit inside the ≤0.18 mm² band (0.152 / 0.051 / 0.180 / 0.028 / 0.017 mm²). Proposed unit is therefore an **oracle correction** in `tests/helpers/scene3d-ring-coverage.js` (measure against the ring's pen opening; report `ringUnreachableMm2` as the complement) with a proof that the corrected oracle can still see a real void — explicitly **no** `surface-fill.js` / `geometry-utils.js` / `fill-boolean.js` change. **The orchestrator has NOT accepted this close: the user judged the picture, and an oracle-side correction turns the metric green without changing the pixels the user objected to** — the judge must rule on whether the visible streaks are genuinely pen-unreachable (in which case the law is unplottable there and the product answer is different from the metric answer) or whether reachable ink is still missing.
- A3 side-finding to keep separate: the plan found a **real robustness bug in `insetMultiPolygon`** (`A3-plan.md` §7). It does not move the F1 numbers and must not be sold as the F1 fix — it needs its own W-id.

- W-27c **iteration 2** (073202a4, fill-audit-d; review resumed → `W-27c-review-2.md`) shipped both of the review's blockers. **Divergence guard:** `sliceAnalyticProjectLocal` now tracks a `best`/`bestAbsF` pair seeded with the untouched input point and accepts a Newton step only if every coordinate is finite, the displacement is within `4 x max(sizes.sx, sizes.sy, sizes.sz, 1)`, and the resulting `|F|` strictly improves on the best seen; the first rejection stops the loop and returns the best point, so the output can never be worse than the input. RED reproduced against a disposable export of 29203162 (61.65 mm displacement at a near-tangent plane, bar <5 mm); GREEN at 073202a4 gives 0 mm — the guard rejects the divergent step outright — with `|F|` never exceeding the input's 0.1025, and a degenerate zero-length plane normal covered. **Non-circular oracle:** a deliberately non-axis-aligned local plane direction (0.35, 0.82, 0.45), forward-rotated by a real yaw25/pitch15/roll10 transform and inverted back through the real `Slices.localPlaneNormal` (with a round-trip sanity test proving the rotation machinery is exercised, not bypassed), checking plane membership by pure dot product — zero reuse of F or the gradient — alongside an independently re-derived surface residual. The 1 mm offset was tuned by mutation: applied to the committed source and reverted, mutation B drives sphere/cone/torus to 0.242 / 0.240 / 0.622 mm against the 0.15 mm bar. 33/33 contour-slice tests, plane-count purity untouched, all guard suites green.
- W-27c **cylinder exemption (documented, needs verification):** cylinder's implicit surface `F = (x/sx)^2 + (z/sz)^2 - 1` has no `y` dependence, so its gradient's y-component is identically zero everywhere and for every plane orientation. The implementer verified that removing the gradient-onto-plane projection changes neither the plane error nor the surface residual for cylinder at every offset from 1 mm to 100 mm — i.e. mutation B is provably undetectable there, not a rig weakness. The cylinder fixture is kept because it still exercises the rotation and non-axis-aligned-plane round-trip. This is an exemption from a blocking requirement, so the reviewer should check the mathematics rather than accept it.

- W-10c **iteration 2** (04ab79bd, fill-audit-c) closed the reviewer's two mandatory followups and produced the round's most important negative finding. Followup 1: the first retrace-coverage test was **self-caught as insensitive** — `localDuty[i]` is computed before the retrace loop, so disabling the loop left it green; it was relabelled honestly (it does verify the duty value and that a real torus arc requests duty > 1) and a genuinely mutation-sensitive guard added beside it, asserting `pathCount > 350` and `inkLen > 1450 mm` on the app-default torus scene (RED with the loop disabled: 293 / 1365.7 mm; GREEN: 409 / 1551.7 mm). Followup 2: a `DUTY_CAP` boundary test that asserts the clamp formula and records the honest finding that **the cap never engages** — max observed `dutyRaw` is 3, and forcing pen to 3x default still tops out at 3, because `pitchFor`'s own floor scales with the same `inkWidth`/`penWidth` pair that sets `PLOT_MIN_PEN`. Followup 3: the `before` pointer now targets 12 freshly-captured genuine 78bbf3e8 images in `after/W-10c/before-78bbf3e8/`, all 12 pairs verified to differ. Tests 9/9 in the file, 53 passed / 1 pre-existing skip across the 7-file targeted suite.
- **W-10c headline finding — the retrace mechanism adds essentially no screen coverage, and the torus wedge got worse.** Rendering the same torus cell with the retrace loop disabled differs from the retrace-enabled render by **0.67% of pixels (2129/320000, max delta 27 — antialiasing jitter)**: retracing an identical curve at an identical radius redraws already-inked pixels, so iteration 1's "+5–8% ink length" was real as a path-length sum but illusory as coverage. Measuring the defect directly (pen width calibrated from a tight-ring region — torus 7 px, cone 2 px — then the fraction of interior pixels in a continuous blank-paper run longer than 2 pen widths) gives **torus 73.9% at 78bbf3e8 → 87.8% at 441af81f, i.e. WORSE**, while cone is 67.2% → 67.6%, unchanged, matching the orchestrator's own visual read. Root cause is structural, not a missing parameter: raising the floor from 0.8x to 1.0x pen mechanically widens the true ring-to-ring gap by ~25% wherever the floor was engaging (~10% of torus angles), and the only way to add real coverage between two floor-widened rings is a ring closer than one full pen — exactly the sub-floor crowding W-10/W-10b/W-10c exist to eliminate. A plausible first hypothesis was disproved on the way: torus has 148/360 = 41.1% of angles that never touch the surface at any radius, but cone has 147/360 = 40.8% and shows no wedge, so the dead zone is normal whenever the highlight origin sits near a silhouette edge, not a torus-topology artefact.

- W-15c **implemented, review in flight** (8bd1581b on 6d6b1b78, fill-audit): the plan's §3.2 shipped verbatim with the owner's **N = 9** ruling. A memoised `isSoloOrientation(record)` predicate (true iff every front face shares one `normalWorld`, `dot >= 0.999`) gates a new `soloDens = round(f.ext / hatchSpacing(d) - 0.5)` term, and `want = min(ceilCount, max(FACET_MIN_RULINGS, soloDens))` — so every object with two or more visible orientations takes the byte-identical old grant and the cross-facet ordering invariants that killed designs A, B and C are untouched **by construction**. RED at 6d6b1b78 `[3,3,3,3]` → GREEN `[6,6,7,9]` at d=1/10/25/50; rendered plane series `3,3,3,3,19,65` → `6,6,7,9,19,65`; on-paper gap at d=50 **8.549 → 3.149 mm** at coverage 0.0950 against `formCeiling('L')` 0.0987, ceiling-bound and plot-safe. No O20/O9/O21 fixture inverted at any point — the `scene3d-facet-tone`, `scene3d-projected-pitch` and `scene3d-faceted-highlight-dispatch` guards passed on the first run with no re-pinning, unlike all three prior designs. - **W-15c's six re-pins all come from one mechanism worth flagging: a ground plane is itself a single-orientation object**, so the new gate opens on any scene whose ground carries `mapper: 'hatch'` with no ground override. That is what moves `scene3d-box-density-bearing`'s `fingerprint(50,'plane')` (`44270f5b:3738` → `10a710a6:3909`), all three `scene3d-hlr-spatial-index-identity` SETTLED rows (129/258 → 208/416, 341/1099 → 404/1225, 560/2303 → 651/2485 paths/points) and both `scene3d-tone-baseline` goldens (`shadow-additive-default` 219 → 284 paths, `shadow-inverse` 58 → 126). In all three spatial-index scenarios only the SETTLED row moves — the DRAFT (`bounds.fastPreview`) row is byte-identical, measured not assumed, consistent with draft omitting ground fill. A grep for the old hashes and counts across `tests/` finds no remaining references, so nothing outside these six moved. **The plan promised every *graded object* byte-identical; the ground plane was never named, so denser ground fill is a user-visible consequence that F-14 did not ask for and the reviewer should rule on**, along with confirming by eye that the two regenerated goldens are right rather than merely rewritten.

- **W-27c new must-track follow-up — cylinder free-axis drift.** Both of the divergence guard's safety checks are blind for a primitive whose `F` does not constrain every coordinate. On an elongated cylinder (`sx=1, sy=50, sz=1` — a plausible long thin tube) with a plane nearly parallel to its axis, from `p=(1.3, 10, 0.1)` (F0 = 0.70), the guard **accepts** excursions along the free Y axis of 1.06 mm, 3.05 mm, 10.13 mm, 30.39 mm and **101.28 mm** as eps shrinks 0.3 → 0.003: `F` stays exactly 0.000000 throughout (correct on the XZ circle), so "strictly reduces |F|" is trivially satisfied at every step, and the `4 x max(sizes)` bound is 200 mm — over twice the cylinder's own 50 mm length — so `dist` never trips it either. The rendered cross-section stays correct; only the free axis drifts silently. Scope needs an aspect ratio around 20:1 **and** a near-axis-parallel cutting plane, so it is narrower than the iteration-1 defect, but the fix must bound the step against the locally relevant dimension rather than the largest one.

(2) The only in-file alternative, geometric inference, is **empirically unreliable on the exact scene that matters**: a flood-fill enclosed-hole detector reads a stable ~40–41% enclosed area for the torus on the F-10 describe block's own off-axis camera at grid sizes 24/32/48/64 (and exactly 0% for cone and sphere every time), but on the **real default-camera gallery scene** it reads **0% at grid sizes 24, 32, 48, 64, 128 and 160**, with a single spurious 2-cell hole (0.02% of on-surface area) at grid 96 alone. The measured unreliability is now pinned as a permanent investigation test (hole fraction < 0.01 at grids 64 and 128 on the real gallery scene) so a future session does not re-spend the budget, and will go red if the substrate ever changes enough for the signal to become viable. Suite 10/10 in that file, 54 passed / 1 pre-existing skip across the 7-file targeted set.

That number already clears the 8° bar on the restated oracle (max interior turn between consecutive points of one emitted ring, excluding each path's own endpoints, since those are genuine occluder cuts), so **the plan's first job is to work out what the user actually circled**: the bar is met while the picture still shows corners, which means either the oracle or the rig is looking at the wrong thing. Related open items already filed separately: the "fan of spikes" degenerate-stub fix (a 0.004 mm 3-point ring inflated to 513 points by `refineSliceRing`), the `sliceRotate = 90°` linking collapse, and cylinder/cone/pyramid open-cap topology; W-27c's own items (d) torus-hole dashed fragments and (e) perf on the 8-dense-sphere fixture also remain untouched.
- **W-25b opened (fill-audit-d, in flight): scope the `SPIRAL_MIN_TURNS` floor.** The W-25 reviewer measured the default buckyball's face rMax at 6.9–8.1 mm against a 14.4 mm threshold at d=50, so the `SPIRAL_MIN_TURNS = 2` floor is **not** a no-op there — the unit must either retune it to thin-cusp faces only, or own the buckyball diff deliberately with a fingerprint test. It also carries the small debt of fixing the stale header comment in `tests/unit/scene3d-mesh-self-occlusion.test.js`.
- **W-27c-0a PLAN-READY and the diagnosis changes the item** (`W-27c-0a-plan.md`, MEASURED, planner read-only, no code changed; runnable probes left in `docs/3d-audit/lane-reports/W-27c-0a-evidence/`). The "angled points" the user circled are **not a turning-angle defect at all** — the 8° bar is met at 7.579° and always will be, so **the oracle was wrong, not the fix.** What the user actually sees is **pen-width ink MERGING where the slice rings converge at the torus's two saddle points**: adjacent rings are drawn closer together than one pen width, so the ink fuses into blobs that read as corners. The plan states two oracles and is explicit that only the second can go RED — O1 (turning angle) passes; **O2 (ink separation) is RED on all five sub-bars**: ink drawn within 0.5 pen widths of other ink totals 124.8 mm, **11.0% of all ink**; ink within 1 pen width is **20.0%** against a ≤5% bar; the same-ring waist narrows to **0.190 mm** at document mm (123.09, 109.07); the largest merged-ink blob is **4.41 x 3.01 mm** against a 3-pen ≈ 0.9 mm bar; and there are **40** merged blobs. Write the RGR test against O2.
- W-27c-0a's fix stays **in lane**: `mappers.js` was re-verified to contain no `contourSlice`/`buildSliceSegments` code (zero matches), so every candidate lands in `scene3d.js` only. **Ranked fix 1 (recommended): a pen-aware crowding cull on the emitted slice runs** in the `contourSlice` emit loop at `scene3d.js:3910-3941` — keep a per-record device-mm occupancy grid at pen resolution filled by already-emitted slice ink, suppress a sample whose nearest already-inked sample is closer than `k · penWidth`, split the run at each suppression boundary, and apply the existing `MIN_RUN_MM` floor to the fragments, **never culling a whole level** (if every run of a ring is suppressed, keep its longest). It attacks the measured cause directly, moves all five O2 sub-bars, needs no new math, and touches neither the cut, the refinement, the projection nor `hlr.js`; `penWidth` is already in scope and emission is plane-ascending, so the cull is a pure function of the documented plane order. **Explicitly out of bounds:** changing what `sliceCount` means or any plane-level position (it would break the plane-count purity tests), `SLICE_REFINE_MAX_ANGLE_DEG`, and any edit to `hlr.js`, `MIN_RUN_MM`, `COLLINEAR_EPS`, `SLICE_SAMPLE_STEP` or `SLICE_CLIP_WORK`. Implementer is queued on fill-audit-d **after W-25b lands**, since they share the worktree.

Worth remembering as the recurring pattern from the previous round: an agent that dies between `git stash` and `git stash pop` is the dangerous case, which is exactly why the protocol bans stash-based red proofs — here, nothing was stashed and nothing needed recovery.

- Unit D phase-align **implemented, review in flight** (8e9b0991 on handoff-c: `shadows.js`, `scene3d-shadow-receive.test.js`, and a new `scripts/unitd-phase-evidence.js`; `hlr.js` was not needed). Root cause found precisely: `hatchRingsEvenOdd`'s numeric-spacing branch computes scan offsets as `pMin + i*sp` from **whichever ring array that particular call received** — the outside call gets `[face].concat(footprintPolys)` while each inside call gets `[fp]` alone, so the two share an angle but derive their phase from completely different bounding boxes. Measured on the two-object fixture (40 mm box over a 320 mm plane, sun az90/el25, hatch/ladder, fillDensity 80, fillAngle 20): outside pitch 8.7228 mm, inside pitch 3.4440 mm, worst phase offset 1.682 mm = **0.1929x the outside pitch** against a ≤0.05x bar. The fix memoises the outside call's angle, spacing and anchor and has each inside call pick an exact integer submultiple `step = outerSpacing / round(outerSpacing / thisSpacing)` continuing from the outside anchor, so every Nth inside ruling lands on an outside ruling's continuation and the rest are exact midlines: worst phase ratio drops to **9.77e-15 (≈0)**, density ratio 3.0x against a ≥1.4x bar, angle mismatch 0. Guards are broad and green — 17/17 in its own file, Unit C 4/4, shadows 18/18, picker 126/126, and 17 further shadow/tone-law files at 190/192 with 2 pre-existing skips — and the flag-OFF path is structurally unreachable because `buildFaceFootprint` returns `null` when `shadowReceiveOn` is false, so the memo is never even populated.
- **Unit D phase-align's mechanism carries a fragility worth tracking regardless of the review verdict:** the fix is a module-level `WeakMap` keyed by ring **object identity**, and it works only because `scene3d.js` passes the same array objects both into the outside `.concat` and later as `[fp]`. A future defensive copy, `.map()` or serialization round-trip on `footprintPolys` would make the memo silently miss and the phase revert to the buggy behaviour **with no test failure**, unless the new test drives the real `scene3d.js` footprint path rather than calling `hatchRingsEvenOdd` with hand-built arrays. The memo is also order-dependent (written only on `rings.length > 1`, read on `rings.length === 1`) and never cleared, so the reviewer should establish what happens if a render reorders or skips the outside call, or if a surviving poly object meets a changed angle or spacing in a later render.
- **A reframing of the Unit D defect worth recording:** the implementer tried a real-browser screenshot first and abandoned it, because the "broken dash" appearance showed up in **both** before and after and proved to be a rasterization/upscale alias — confirmed by dumping the real app's `g.scenePaths`, where every receiver fill path is a single unbroken 2-point chord in both states. The phase irregularity the judge measured was therefore real, but the "reads as a patch of broken dashes" description was partly an artifact of how the evidence was viewed. The final evidence renders the emitted paper-space paths directly as crisp SVG through a real Chromium page instead. Also noted: `scripts/unitd-phase-evidence.js` **swaps `shadows.js` on disk** to obtain its genuine before and claims to restore it unconditionally with an on-disk verification after every run — the failure and exception paths should be checked, since a committed script that mutates a source file is a footgun for the next session. And this is the **fourth gallery coverage gap** logged today: no cell exercises `shadowReceiveOnObjects`, so the evidence is necessarily a bespoke scene.

The orchestrator cropped `after/W-27c-0a` torus med at **full resolution** and found the crowding cull **cutting short mid-ring breaks, splitting several rings in the lower half — the W-27c item 0(b) micro-gap defect reintroduced**, in the same lane that had fixed it at 767bed54. Neither the review nor the (b) micro-gap test caught it: the cull's deliberate run-splitting at suppression boundaries is a *different* source of fragmentation from the gap-clipping the (b) guard was written against, and — the sharp part — **that guard's fragment ceiling had itself been re-pinned 55 → 80 precisely to accommodate this splitting**, so it had been tuned out of the way of the defect it would otherwise have caught. **Iteration 3 is directed: (1) whole-ring / whole-run culling only, never splitting a run mid-ring; (2) extend the micro-gap oracle to cover cull-created breaks, RED at 61ff00cb.** The fragment ceiling should also come back down once splitting stops. Lane fill-audit-d is **not** free.
- **Process lesson, logged as an incident: a metric-green / picture-red case, and the clearest one of this session.** W-27c-0a iteration 2b passed three independent checks — the implementer's own five-sub-bar sweep, the reviewer's from-scratch re-measurement, and the (b) micro-gap guard — and carried 49/49 green tests, honest floors+10% bands, per-primitive pass/fail reporting and an ACCEPT-PARTIAL verdict. The defect was found only by looking at a **full-resolution crop** rather than the 800 px gallery cell. Two rules worth carrying forward: **harness-clean is still not app-clean**, and **a guard whose bar you widen to accommodate a new mechanism stops guarding against that mechanism** — the 55 → 80 re-pin was disclosed, justified and reviewed, and it still cost exactly the coverage that mattered.

It was found by looking at the render, fixed, and O1 then dropped to the true curvature-only figure of 0.127 mm. The lesson for the review and for T2–T4: **O1 as written cannot distinguish genuine curvature from a defect**, so the question is not whether the number passes but what would now fail if a kink returned.
- T1's open questions: the implementer argues O1/O2's shortfall is structural, not effort — the plan's RED came from *internal* frame access, the file's existing `nearestRulingTangent` proxy saturates at a spurious 90° on **both** trees (pre-fix measures 83% over 10° by that proxy against the plan's bounded internal 44.77°), and genuine surface curvature over a 2.25–8.7 mm tick arm legitimately carries a chord more than 10° from a flat reference, since "a metric built against a flat reference cannot simultaneously reward curvature and demand near-zero deviation from a flat line". **If that argument is accepted, O1 and O2 as written are the wrong oracles and should be restated rather than recorded as missed.** O2 was already restated once — "p99 ≤ 10°" became "fraction of marks over 10° ≤ 0.01", claimed exactly equivalent, then shipped at ≤0.20 — so the equivalence and the 0.20 floor both want confirming. A structural pin also changed, `expect(pp.length).toBe(2)` → `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2)`, which is correct because the 2-point pin *was* defect D1, but it leaves **no upper bound on a mark's point count** — an unbounded walk would now pass. And O4's shortfall was deliberately left to a later unit per the plan's §9 stop conditions (truncating more ink versus widening the drop floor is not T1's call), which needs assigning to T2 or T3 before it becomes nobody's.

- Unit D phase-align CLOSED **DONE/FU** (8e9b0991, handoff-c; reviewer **ACCEPT-WITH-FOLLOWUPS** with the RGR, the flag-OFF byte-identity, an identity-mutation case and a two-face case all independently verified). **The `WeakMap`-by-object-identity fragility flagged by the secretary is answered by construction rather than argued away**: the reviewer cloned the `footprintPolys` elements before the outside `.concat` call, breaking the identity link the memo depends on, and the same Unit D polish test — plus the RED-proof pin — **fails at the identical phase ratio as the pre-fix baseline**, because the test drives the real `generate()` pipeline and measures the emitted geometry's actual phase rather than the WeakMap's internal state. It is an outcome-based regression test, not a snapshot of the mechanism, so no re-keying is needed for coverage. The order-dependence concern is theoretical only: `buildFaceFootprint` builds fresh arrays on every call, so a stale anchor cannot leak between renders through any current call site. And the evidence script's on-disk swap is **double-guarded** — an inner `finally` restores immediately after the BEFORE render and an outer `finally` unconditionally re-writes and verifies the fixed content before exit — with the worktree's `shadows.js` confirmed to hold the fix. **Lane handoff-c is free again**; U9 still waits on the U1→U8 chain.
- Unit D phase-align's four follow-ups, none requiring rework: **(1)** non-directional lights (area, point, spot) drive shadow-receive direction from `azimuth`/`elevation` regardless of `light.type`, defaulting to 135°/45° when absent — pre-existing, neither introduced nor worsened here, but it **needs its own W-id before anyone ships a point or spot shadow-receive scene**; **(2)** consider carrying the phase and angle on the footprint record itself instead of (or alongside) the identity-keyed `WeakMap`, purely for robustness against a future defensive-copy refactor — not required now, since the outcome-based test already catches that regression; **(3)** `scripts/unitd-phase-evidence.js`'s swap-and-restore has no protection against a hard process kill mid-swap, which would leave the pre-fix file on disk with no `finally` able to run — add a one-line header warning (inherent to the technique, not specific to this script); **(4)** a doc fix — the code comment and report claim the original branch is "byte-for-byte untouched", which should read "unchanged apart from a cosmetic loop-variable rename".
- **W-30 filed (handoff-c, `shadows.js`, QUEUED after U9): shadow-receive direction ignores `light.type`.** Pre-existing and not introduced by Unit D — its reviewer surfaced it. Area, point and spot lights all drive the shadow-receive direction from `azimuth`/`elevation` regardless of `light.type`, **defaulting to 135°/45° when those are absent**, so a point or spot light casts its receive-shadow in a direction unrelated to where the light actually sits. The fix needs **per-light-type direction**: point and spot from the light **position** to the receiver, area **sampled** across the emitter, with **an RGR case per light type**. U9 also lands on handoff-c and must go first. Two practical notes: `shadowReceiveOnObjects` is default OFF, and **no gallery cell exercises it**, so evidence will need a bespoke scene in the style Unit D phase-align built. This is blocking for anyone shipping a point or spot shadow-receive scene — worth doing before that happens rather than after.

- **F1-placement PLAN-READY, and the plan overturns the routing F1 has carried all session** (`F1-placement-plan.md`, STATUS MEASURED — mechanism found, located to file:line, prototyped twice with before/after numbers). F1 is a **ruling-placement defect, not a "between ribbon stretches" defect, and it is not pen-unreachable** — and it is **the same mechanism as W-26's root cause**. For the five F1 laws the fill is a family of rulings subset off a fixed master grid by a Sturmian phase accumulator, and for three of them the coverage driving that accumulator has **no radiance term at all**: `weightCovAt` (`surface-fill.js:4273`) falls through to `weightBaseCov()` (`:4303`) whose `isWaveLaw()` branch returns **`wvFlatCov()` (`:3910`) — a function that takes no arguments**, `masterPitch / (wvPitchPen() * inkWidth())`, one number for the whole object. It reaches the drop decision via `algoCoverage` → `covAtSample` → `spanDrops` → `ladderKeeps` → **`ladderStep` (`:1289`)**, seeded at `LADDER_PHASE0 = 0.5`. On the default torus at Density 50 `c = 0.44803`, so the kept-index gap multiset is **{2×7, 3×2}** — one gap in four is **50% wider than its neighbours with literally zero tone behind it**. Compounding it, the accumulator advances **one grid index per ruling and never by screen distance**, even though `covAtSample` already holds the projection-correct `localPitch` and passes it to `weightCovAt`, which ignores it. Together these put a **41.7 × 6.6 mm, 62.63 mm² bare strip** across the lower front of the torus — **5% of the visible surface and 30x the entire `ringNotInkMm2` residue A3 measured** — and the largest cluster is unambiguous: **77 dropped-ruling samples inside it, 0 kept-ruling samples.**
- F1-placement's two measured prototypes and the ranking: **Fix 1 is LOCAL (Prototype B) — recommended** — state the flat reserve against `localPitch` instead of the global `masterPitch` in `weightCovAt`'s wave fallthrough, guarded to `isWaveLaw() && !isWv6()`, so the accumulator advances by **screen distance** (the same quantity `emitContFamily`'s walk integrates) without routing through it; express it as a named `wvPlaceCov(localPitch)` beside `wvFlatCov()` so the latter's three other consumers stay untouched, and fall back to `weightBaseCov()` when `localPitch` is not finite. **Prototype A (SNAP)** — snap the flat reserve to a reciprocal integer `c → 1/round(1/c)` — wins the **area** bar (largest cluster 22.77 vs B's 45.80 mm²) but as prototyped inside `wvFlatCov()` it **leaks into the WV6 laws** (`ampSpacing` ink **+10.8%**, `weaveDepth` blank worse), so a shippable SNAP must live in a new `wvPlaceCov()` used only by the placement branch. **B wins the depth bar by a mile** (`>2.0 mm` blank 0.28 / 0.00 / 0.00 mm² against A's 1.98 / 1.78 / 1.94, from a RED of 11.89 / 14.70 / 12.15). **Neither prototype reaches zero** — the largest bare cluster is still 22–46 mm² and 11–30 mm long, and §9's sibling unit **F1-amp** owns the rest.

Anti-vacuity is gated the same way `scene3d-ribbon-f1b-streaks.test.js` uses `VECTURA_PRE_F1B`, plus `wide > 0` and `degenerate === 0` per subject so no bar is met by thinning the ribbon.

This seems off."* Evidence: a sphere at Type=Crosshatch, Fill Style=Ladder, Rung detail=Fine rungs, Angle 45°, Density 50 renders ~22 evenly spaced tilted bands and **no crossing family at all**; the orchestrator's montage counts cylinder ≈ 30 primary vs ≈ 5 crossing, ellipsoid ≈ 25 vs ≈ 4, T1 sphere ≈ 24 vs ≈ 8, against the pre-audit v1.3.98 gallery cell `shots/A/sphere__crosshatch__ladder__med__a.webp` at ≈ 22 vs ≈ 20 — **so this is a regression introduced between v1.3.98 and the merge, not a long-standing gap.** **Secretary's read of the code (read-only, no edits):** the cause is almost certainly **W-26b-1's cross-share change, not the Fine-rungs cascade and not a picker/param mismatch.** `CROSS_SHARE_BASE = 0.1` (`src/core/scene3d/surface-fill.js:4652`) feeds `crossShareOf` (`:4657-4660`) and `ladderCrossWantedPitch` (`:4661-4665`), so at the shipped default `crossDensityRatio = 1` the crossing family's coverage target is `cA × 0.1` and its wanted pitch is `masterPitch / cB` — about **10× family A's pitch by construction**, i.e. roughly one crossing line per ten primary rulings. Where `cB` bottoms out at `LADDER_COV_MIN = 0.02` (`:4597`) or the `CROSS_DFMAX_BOOST_CAP = 20` step ceiling binds (`:9718-9720`), the crossing family can fall to **zero drawn rulings inside the silhouette** — exactly Jay's sphere. Ruled out: the crossing family's line budget is untouched at ratio 1 (`Math.max(2, Math.round(count / crossRatio))`, `:10771-10773`), and Fine rungs adds tone rungs rather than placement (`isEvenLadder()` at `:4559` routes `fineLadder` down the same continuous path). **The tension a planner must resolve:** `CROSS_SHARE_BASE` was set well under a 50/50 split deliberately, to stop two crossed families compounding to `1-(1-c)^2` saturation — the W-26b-1 C1 fix that took cylinder D220 from 9326.7 → **5153.7 mm (+4.9% vs pre-W-26)**. Restoring line *parity* must not restore the saturation; the honest shape is near-parity in **count** with the two families sharing one plot-safety/coverage budget, not two independently-full-coverage families. **RGR (as ruled): the crossing family's drawn line count must equal the hatch family's within ±10% on sphere / cylinder / torus × ladder × d = 1 / 50 / 220, unless the law documents a deliberate asymmetry or the deviation lies in a highlight/shadow zone; RED on Jay's scene** (sphere, crosshatch, ladder, fine rungs, 45°, d=50) and on the three montage cells, with a guard that cylinder D220 crosshatch ink stays in the C1 band rather than returning to 9326.7 mm. Evidence cells (all verified present in `manifest.A.*.jsonl`): `{sphere,cylinder,ellipsoid}__crosshatch__ladder__{low,med,max}__a` into `after/W-36/`, plus `sphere__{hatch,contour}__ladder__med__a` as must-not-move controls. **NEEDS A PLANNER** before an implementer starts; it must NOT be folded into W-31's brief — W-36 is a count/share regression, W-31 is spacing evenness within each family, and W-31's cell-shape measurement is meaningless while family B carries a tenth of the lines. **W-31 is to be re-measured after W-36 lands.**

- **W-30c's four real defects, three of them user-visible, and one of them is a shipped feature that silently does nothing.** **R1 — an area light loses ALL softening when occluded:** the gate at `regions.js:176` is evaluated once, for the emitter's centre, **before** the N-sample loop, so a 32 mm penumbra collapses to 0 mm (max intensity error 0.3985). This is Unit D's long-standing gate, now measured. **R2 — a non-convex caster's footprint fills its own hole**, because the receive path reduces the caster to `Shadows.convexHull` (`scene3d.js:1434`): the torus hole is **22.08 mm** (directional) / **24.14 mm** (positional), and a ray-cast cross-check on a 4 mm grid finds **35.5% of the point-light footprint's area and 45.8% of the directional footprint's area is not actually occluded** — two orders of magnitude over a pen width. **R3 — and that makes the torus shadow vanish entirely**, because the footprint's tone is sampled at exactly ONE point, the polygon centroid, on the stated assumption that a hard shadow is binary; for a hull-with-a-hole **the centroid lands in the hole**, `pointInShadow` returns false, and the inside pass gets unshadowed spacing while the even-odd outside pass has already excluded the footprint. Density ratio **1.00 vs a sphere's 3.02**: **a torus casting onto a flat face produces no visible receive shadow at all**, in both the pre-W-30 directional and the post-W-30b positional paths. **R4 — an `ambient` light at index 0 deletes a point light's receive shadow** (3.02× → 1.00×), because the footprint is built for `lights[0]` whatever its type while the tone is computed multi-light. **Measured and dismissed:** the spot cone is ignored by the footprint builder, but a caster outside the cone always projects harmlessly.
- **`area` is byte-identical to `point` in every W-30c row** — `projectLightToPlane` (`shadows.js:409-418`) treats an area light as a single position, so an area light's receive footprint has a hard edge and no penumbra. W-30 and W-30b each disclosed this as "matches the ground-shadow precedent", which was fair; **§1c is what changes the verdict, by showing the *shading* half of the same gap is a real defect (R1) rather than a documented simplification.** Worth remembering: the same simplification can be acceptable on one side of a feature and a bug on the other.

- **W-10d-2's plan came back PLAN-BLOCKED-ON-RULING (`W-10d-2-plan.md`), and the block was correct: this ledger's own 2026-09-05 contract would have destroyed user data.** The planner swept all **35 `PICKER_IDS` × 10 primitives × 8 mappers = 2800 combinations** and found **2014 / 2800 (71.9%) have `isReachableOn === false`**, of which **1365 sit in contexts where `ladder` itself is unreachable** — every primitive under `none`/`wireframe`/`contourSlice`, every faceted primitive under `contour`/`spiral`/`stipple` — so **68% of the unreachable set has no fallback to write back to at all.** Worse, the gate is not a no-op gate: faceted+hatch (`box`/`hatch`/`contourFlow`) and cap-limited solids do render identically to `ladder`, but curved+spiral, curved+stipple, pyramid+spiral and the torus gate render **visibly differently** — `torus`/`hatch`/`originSpiral` is **556 paths / 5851 points against ladder's 135 / 910**. **A blanket `isReachableOn`-keyed write-back would therefore silently rewrite the stored fill style of MOST objects in the corpus and materially change every curved+spiral/stipple document on load.** The planner also confirmed that after W-10d and W-10d-3, **no UI or canvas lie remains** — write-back does not close a lie, it changes both sides in step.
- **STANDING NOTE, and the most reusable thing to come out of this unit: `isReachableOn` means "too degraded to be worth offering", NOT "draws nothing different".** W-03's own comment says it out loud — the 36 laws it added to `CURVED_SPIRAL_STIPPLE_INERT` are "bare centreline … 18-19 distinct pictures across 39 laws", not byte-identical to Ladder. **Any future unit tempted to use this gate as a "safe to substitute" predicate must re-read this note first.** The 2026-09-05 ruling made exactly that mistake, it sat in the ledger unchallenged for four days, and it was caught only because a planner measured the gate instead of trusting its name.
- **RULING: W-10d-2 ships CONTRACT A — CURATED, and it is IN-FLIGHT on fill-audit-2 at `66d9092d`** (with the `params.js` disjointness proof against U9's `clampShadowToneLaw` block still mandatory). That combination's render **changes deliberately** (it is the repair W-10c and W-10d both deferred) and **carries a CHANGELOG line**; **everything else stays byte-identical**, proven by a blast-radius negative: `sphere` + `spiral` + `taperedEnds` must come back unchanged in both the stored bag and the canvas. **No undo entry at load.** Guard **G1 asserts the curated table has exactly one entry**, so the set cannot widen unnoticed — which, given how this unit's first contract went, is the assertion that matters most. **Contract B (render-safe) rejected**: byte-identical by construction, but it *excludes the torus case* and those classes have no UI lie to fix, so the user value is ~zero. **Contract C (withdraw) rejected**: the torus repair is owed.

Only sphere camera b passes.
- **W-36 is explicitly NOT the cause of W-31 and must not be re-opened** — the planner proved it rather than asserting it: per-family gap medians are **identical** between the crosshatch pair and a single family (sphere 0.477/0.464, cylinder 0.634/0.603, ellipsoid 0.407/0.402), and W-36's own P2 whole-cell median is satisfied. **W-36 turned "there are no cells" into "the cells are square on average and wrong locally"** — which is precisely Jay's sentence. It also confirms quantitatively that W-31 really was unmeasurable before W-36: family B's few surviving rulings were 7:1 slivers.
- **W-31's root cause, class (c-1), with file:line — and it is the P0 ladder-gap rule generalised.** `probe()` returns `mmPerFrac` as an **area-weighted mean over the WHOLE ruling** (`surface-fill.js:9766`), and the walk then takes **one scalar step** for that ruling (`df = clamp(want / pb.mmPerFrac, dfMin, dfMax)` at `:9899`, emitted at a constant parameter offset at `:9955`). So the achieved local gap is `want × perpPitch_local / perpPitch_meanOfRuling`: a family whose local clearance varies ~2× along its rulings gets ~2× gap variation, the crossing family's clearance field is its mirror, and **the cell aspect varies ~4×**. Measured confirmation with `want` pinned flat at 0.75 mm: family A delivers 0.429 → 0.828 mm while family B delivers 0.820 → 0.421 mm across the same cylinder. **The variation the walk cannot see is the variation INSIDE each ruling** — which is exactly W-26 R1a's rule, moved from ruling centroids down to local geometry, as the handoff always said W-31 would be.
- **W-31's secondary mechanism (c-2) is direction-dependent tone integration, and it rules out a whole class of fix.** `probe()` returns the ruling's area-weighted **mean** intensity and `want` is computed once per ruling, so two families at 45° and 135° integrate the same tone field along different directions and disagree by up to 4.2× on `want` p95; the `dfMax` ceiling then bites only the family that asked wide (10.8% vs 0.0%). **Any per-ruling scalar tone target is direction-dependent — this cannot be fixed by a better average, which was measured, not assumed. Only a per-sample target removes it.**

The plan discloses — deliberately — that Rank 1 was NOT prototyped**, while Rank 2 and Rank 3 both were, and while **W-36's plan shipped a prototyped Rank 1**. That asymmetry is the reason the spike is mandatory rather than advisory. **If the spike clears the cylinder band, take Rank 1** (per-sample "warped" offset placement gated to the crosshatch pair: ruling i+1 becomes the offset curve of ruling i at the locally-wanted clearance, so the cell is square by construction, and it discharges (c-2) for free by making the tone target per-sample). **If it does not, take Rank 3** — land the cell-shape test as a **non-regression ceiling at today's numbers**, nothing tightened and nothing widened, and file the placement rewrite as **W-31b**. **Never take Rank 2** (per-sample tone only): it was prototyped and **rejected by measurement**; do not retry it.

- **W-33's real find is structural and the plan did not predict it: refinement ran BEFORE the seam join, and a join vertex is the tail's last point / head's first point — structurally EXEMPT from each half's own open-polyline turn check.** Refining before the join is therefore *provably* incomplete, not merely imprecise. The fix defers refinement: each unsplit piece carries a per-point `tt` array spliced across the join exactly like the existing `__hw` ribbon-width array, and refinement runs after joining, so the join vertex is checked as the ordinary interior vertex it has become. **A sub-bug surfaced while fixing it:** the decreasing-`tt` wraparound correction was gated on `closed === true`, but a **seam-joined OPEN piece needs the identical correction at its internal seam** without the whole piece being closed. Un-gating it (apply whenever `t1 < t0`) fixed it — and it was caught only because the naive un-wrapped average put an inserted point **on the wrong side of the sphere: 178° at one point, a 47 mm jump across the form**, in a scratch run.

It was found by direct instrumentation of `firstOccludedSample`'s hit/null outcome (immune to the camera artifacts that produced a false signal in a cruder earlier version of the same check), and **the impl report characterises the commit as "the fix" without noting the new ceiling** — the original bug failed even at the tested 135:1 rig, so the improvement is real and substantial; it is the framing that overstated it.
- **Why the ceiling is acceptable rather than a blocker: it is not reachable through ordinary use.** The UI's own torus size slider caps `sx` at 200 (`scene3d-panel.js`'s `D()`/`sizeRange` default), so a slider-driven torus has `major ≤ 150` — comfortably inside the confirmed-good range. Reaching the new failure point requires `sx` in the 620–650+ range (major 465–490 mm+), which means **a hand-edited or imported `.vectura` carrying an out-of-slider parameter, or compounding with the object's own `transform.scale` multiplier**. Worth remembering that both of those paths exist, though: an imported file is exactly how a user would encounter it without ever touching the slider.

A `HATCH_LAW_RECIPES.penStipple` entry now translates its "shorten the marks" mechanism into hatch terms using the same `hatchEndTrim` device `taperedEnds` and `endShorten` already use, and the now-dead `DOT_LAW_RECIPES.penStipple` is **kept and commented rather than deleted**, preserving the record of the pre-move design intent. **A picker re-categorisation is never only a picker change when a second surface dispatches on the same field.**

- **T4 is CLOSED DONE/FU at `a3b651f0`** — reviewer **ACCEPT-WITH-FOLLOWUPS** (`T4-review.md`), with **15 numbers reproduced independently.** ⚠ **The widened bar is ruled, and the ruling is worth more than a verdict: 3.5 mm is NOT the mathematically minimal passing value — 3.0 already clears 0.85, barely — but it IS the principled one.** It is derived from the mechanism's own worst-case geometric offset (`old bar 2.5 + MK_BAND_MAX_PASSES × inkWidth / 2 = 2.5 + 1.008 ≈ 3.51 mm`) rather than reverse-engineered from the smallest number that passes, and **the derivation predates knowing the fraction curve** — it lands near the 3.0 break-even by a coincidence of the geometry, not by tuning to pass. It also **cannot mask a placement defect elsewhere**: d=med is saturated at 1.0 at every radius tested (2.0–5.0 mm), so the widening reaches **only the sparse d=1 cell** where the band legitimately enters deep ramp states, and the file's other `nearestRulingTangent` call is untouched. **That is the standard a widened bar has to meet in this audit — a derivation, a bounded blast radius, and a reason the old value was wrong rather than merely inconvenient.**

Scope: a **floor + ±10% band on sphere/hatch/mkDashRamp d=220 ink through the creation-defaults pipeline**, with **the floor set BELOW the measured drift envelope so it is not a coin bar** — the W-26b-3 / U9-2 shape rather than the lighter one-sided margin. ⚠ **Measure the envelope before choosing the numbers**: a bar pinned at 1501.1 with a ±10% band alone would fail on ordinary fixture or platform drift while still passing a genuine regression all the way down to 1351 mm — which is precisely the coin-bar failure this audit has already fixed twice.
